## AGENTS.md Protection

This file contains permanent project instructions.

Codex MUST NOT modify AGENTS.md unless the user explicitly requests
or approves the modification.

Codex may automatically maintain:
- codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md
- codex-docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md
- codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md
- codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Mandatory Documentation Maintenance

Documentation updates are part of the implementation and are NOT optional.

After every implementation, Codex MUST determine whether the change affects
architecture, trading behavior, operational behavior, or previous technical
decisions.

### Architecture changes
If the implementation changes any of the following:
- component responsibilities
- data flow
- database/table ownership
- event flow
- WebSocket usage
- service dependencies
- application boundaries
- ingestion architecture
- Replay/Production architecture

Codex MUST update:

docs/ARCHITECTURE.md

The architecture documentation must describe the FINAL implemented state,
not only what was originally planned.

### Every completed fix/change
Codex MUST update:

docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md

Include:
- date
- problem
- root cause
- solution
- files/classes changed
- database impact
- Production impact
- Replay impact

### Trading behavior changes
Codex MUST update:

docs/TRADING_RULES.md

### Investigation findings
Important investigation findings and rejected approaches MUST be recorded in:

docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md

## Definition of Done

A task is NOT COMPLETE until all required documentation has been updated.

Before finishing a task, Codex MUST explicitly check:
"Does this change require documentation updates?"

Do not wait for the user to ask for documentation updates.


## Trade investigations
When asked to investigate a trade:

- Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
- Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
- Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
- Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
- Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  timestamps as UTC.
- Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
- Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
- Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
- Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
- Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
- Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
- State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
- Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  an investigation unless the user requests a fix.

Report:
1. What happened.
2. Why the system entered and exited.
3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.

## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)

The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.

- Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
  `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
- The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
  connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
- Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
- Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
  not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
- `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
  `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
  trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
  policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.

## Local-candle implementation safety

When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:

- Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
  `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
  disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
  otherwise.
- Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
  order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
- A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
  event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
  execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
- Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
  overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
  open-to-closed transition.
- A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
  not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
  an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
  finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
  existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
  data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
  gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
  create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
  or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
  bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
- Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
  manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
  Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
  explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
  to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
  unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
  silently stuck pending signal.
- Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
  duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
  while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
  are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
  retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
  outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
- Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
  participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
  or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
  new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
  to finish before shutdown.
- Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
  wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
  possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
  durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
  atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
  must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
  callers.
- Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
  before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
  excluded from live-latency monitoring.
- Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
  age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
  accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
  effect needs a separate idempotent design.
- Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
  block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
  that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
  distinct from persisted trading fences.
- OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
  tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
- Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
  for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
  excluded.

## Delivery discipline

- Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
  migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
  Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
- Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
  explicitly requests it.
- Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
  an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
  deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.

## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation

FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
local-candle change or describe it as deployed.

Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
BTC protection remains unchanged.

- Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
  `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
- A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
  5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
  hard-veto.
- Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
  `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
  means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
- A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
  quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
- Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
  applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
- Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
  prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.

# MANDATORY PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE CONTRACT

This section extends the existing governance. It does not replace any earlier
project, investigation, architecture, trading, safety, or documentation rule.

For any material code change, implementation is forbidden until the team has
produced and approved the same exact revision of:

`agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`

The specification is the implementation contract and MUST contain, at minimum:

1. Business objective and the exact agreed business line/flow.
2. Confirmed root cause and evidence.
3. In-scope and out-of-scope boundaries.
4. Complete class/file change registry (modify/create/delete/rename/config/
   migration/test/documentation).
5. Class-by-class and material method-by-method current behavior -> agreed new
   behavior, reason, inputs/outputs, side effects, error handling, concurrency,
   transactionality, Replay impact and Production impact.
6. Mandatory logging registry covering all materially affected areas. Logs must
   make the business flow and decisions reconstructable without exposing
   credentials/secrets. Include relevant correlation/trade/symbol/interval/
   candle/test identifiers and respect the project's UTC/KSA rules.
7. Database, configuration and trading-behavior registries, explicitly stating
   NONE when there is no impact.
8. Test and validation registry, including positive, negative, regression,
   failure/recovery, concurrency where relevant, Replay, and Production-safe
   validation.
9. Risks, rollback plan, observability plan and documentation registry.
10. Master implementation registry assigning an ID to every authorized change,
    log, DB/config item, test and documentation update.

No source/config/database/trading change may be implemented unless it is
registered in the approved specification. No unrelated refactoring or cleanup
may be hidden inside an approved fix.

## Dual-agent specification approval

The SAME exact specification revision must be fully reviewed and explicitly
approved by BOTH agents:

- Codex: APPROVED
- Claude: APPROVED
- Unresolved material disagreements: 0

There is no majority vote and no fixed review-iteration limit. Any material
revision invalidates both prior approvals and requires a complete re-review by
both agents. Even after dual approval, implementation remains NOT AUTHORIZED
until the user explicitly approves that exact revision.

## Code implementation direction

After explicit user approval:

- Codex is the implementation owner.
- Claude is the mandatory independent code reviewer.
- Claude reviews the COMPLETE implementation, not only the latest patch.
- Claude comments/rejects; Codex corrects or disputes with technical evidence;
  Claude re-reviews the complete updated implementation.
- Repeat for as many iterations as required. There is no fixed iteration cap.
- Codex cannot self-approve its implementation and the Coordinator cannot
  overrule a material Claude objection.
- Any implementation-code change after Claude approval invalidates that
  approval and requires another complete Claude review.
- A material deviation from the approved specification stops implementation,
  reopens dual-agent design review, creates a new specification revision, and
  requires renewed user approval.

## Post-implementation registry reconciliation

Before technical acceptance, reconcile the actual implementation against the
approved master registry. Every planned item must be accounted for and every
actual change must map to an approved registry ID.

Acceptance requires at minimum:

- missing registered changes: NONE
- unregistered implementation changes: NONE
- unresolved material review findings: 0
- required logging implemented and reviewed
- required tests/validation completed with evidence
- Replay/Production parity reviewed where applicable
- required documentation updates completed
- Claude final full-code review: APPROVED
- Codex final verification of the exact Claude-approved state: PASSED

# POST-CONSENSUS PERSISTENT KNOWLEDGE

After an investigation reaches evidence-backed convergence, durable agreed
facts are candidates for `agent-docs/AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md`. Candidate text must
be the SAME exact revision reviewed by both Codex and Claude and may be written
there only when BOTH return AGREED. Rejected hypotheses, unresolved disputes,
speculation, and proposed-but-not-implemented architecture must not be stored
as confirmed current state.

After an approved implementation is fully reviewed and validated, update the
appropriate Codex and Claude project-memory documents and the shared agreed
knowledge/decisions so future sessions do not start from zero. These updates
must preserve history: supersede old facts/decisions rather than silently
rewriting them.

For Coordinator-driven team work, maintain `agent-docs/LIVE_TEAM_STATUS.md` and the current run's `LIVE_EVENTS.log` so the user can follow the engineering process. Show actions, evidence, findings, challenges, disagreements, status changes, and next actions. Do not expose or request private chain-of-thought. When an agent is silent during a long call, a heartbeat may report elapsed time and the declared task only; never manufacture progress.

# DIRECT AGENT -> COORDINATOR HANDOFF

Codex or Claude may begin work directly with the user. When the work reaches a governance gate or the user asks to hand it to the Coordinator, the direct agent MUST preserve its current artifact/findings and invoke or instruct the Coordinator using the direct-handoff interface rather than proceeding alone.

Required handoff metadata:
- origin: CODEX or CLAUDE
- type: GENERAL, INVESTIGATION, TECHNICAL_DESIGN, or CODE_REVIEW
- exact artifact/file when one exists
- concise current status and unresolved questions

From Coordinator acceptance onward, `agent-docs/LIVE_TEAM_STATUS.md` and the run's `LIVE_EVENTS.log` are the human-readable team view. The dashboard must identify the direct-agent origin and imported context. Pre-handoff work is imported as context only and must never be fabricated as historical live events.

A direct-agent handoff does not bypass independent review, dual-agent approval, the frozen specification gate, user approval, or the Codex-implements / Claude-reviews implementation loop.

## AI team conversational command aliases (additive)

For direct Codex or Claude sessions in this repository, load
`agent-docs/COMMANDS.md` when the user asks `team commands`, `proceed claude`,
`proceed claude deep`, `proceed codex`, `proceed coordinator`, or any command
listed there. Treat these as plain-language command aliases; do not claim they
are native slash commands unless the installed client confirms registration.

- `proceed claude`: perform ONE actual Claude CLI consultation and display the
  captured Claude response in this direct conversation; no implied approval.
- `proceed coordinator`: invoke the existing coordinator with a real handoff
  artifact and show the run path; its live dashboard begins at handoff.
- Preserve all existing investigation, memory, safety, and approval gates.
- If a command cannot execute, state why rather than fabricating a result.

## Executable direct peer/team commands - consolidated latest

When the user uses the commands below, execute the local script rather than merely describing what should happen. Never fabricate a peer answer.

- `proceed claude`: from direct Codex, run `scripts/team-peer.ps1 -Peer CLAUDE` with the current bounded question/artifact and display Claude's actual captured output in the same Codex conversation. This is one peer consultation only.
- `proceed claude deep`: same, with `-Deep` for maximum configured Claude effort and public research.
- `proceed codex`: mirror one-shot Codex consultation from direct Claude.
- `proceed codex deep`: mirror deep Codex consultation.
- `proceed coordinator`: run `scripts/team-handoff.ps1` with the direct agent as origin and the current artifact. The formal multi-round Coordinator workflow and live dashboard start at handoff.
- `team commands`: display `agent-docs/COMMANDS.md`.

Use `scripts/team-command.ps1` as the common local dispatcher when convenient. A one-shot peer consultation is NOT dual-agent consensus, specification approval, user authorization, or permission to change code.

## Frozen human-review PDFs

Major approval artifacts use Markdown as the machine-readable source and a frozen PDF as the human-review artifact. Follow `agent-docs/PDF_ARTIFACT_RULES.md` and use `scripts/freeze-agent-artifact.ps1`.

Required when the corresponding stage exists:
- FINAL_TECHNICAL_REVIEW-Rxx.pdf
- FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION-Rxx.pdf
- IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT-Rxx.pdf
- VALIDATION_REPORT-Rxx.pdf

The PDF must identify the exact revision, SHA-256 of the source Markdown, Codex verdict, Claude verdict, and implementation-authorization state. Any material source edit invalidates the frozen revision and requires both agents to review the new revision again. PDF generation never authorizes implementation.

## Evidence-driven round continuation

A configured MaxRounds value is an emergency/cost ceiling only. It is never a consensus criterion. `MaxRounds=0` means no fixed coordinator round ceiling. If a positive ceiling is reached with material disagreement, status is CONSENSUS NOT ACHIEVED and implementation remains blocked. Continue only through a new/continued formal run with preserved evidence; never manufacture agreement.

## Post-consensus structured knowledge harvest

After formal convergence and dual approval of the exact technical review, the Coordinator must harvest a structured candidate knowledge package from the completed evidence. Candidate categories may include CONFIRMED_IMPLEMENTATION, CONFIRMED_BEHAVIOR, CONFIRMED_ARCHITECTURE, CONFIRMED_TRADING_RULE, DATABASE_ENTITY_FACTS, OPERATIONAL_LOG_FACTS, HISTORICAL_FACT, and REJECTED_ASSUMPTIONS. The SAME exact candidate text must be independently approved by Codex and Claude before it enters AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md. Proposed future design is never recorded as implemented architecture.

## Executable implementation review loop

`scripts/implementation-review.ps1` is the guarded automation entry point for the approved code phase. It MUST refuse to run unless explicit `-UserAuthorized` is supplied for the exact frozen specification. Codex is implementer; Claude is mandatory independent full reviewer. Claude never edits implementation in this loop. Rejection returns to Codex correction, then Claude reviews the complete updated implementation again. A material design deviation stops the loop and requires a revised specification plus renewed dual-agent and user approval. No deployment is authorized by this script.

# JEAN FORMAL REVIEW MODE (ADDITIVE)

The User may act as the sole formal reviewer for a task. This mode is explicit and task-scoped.

## Command: `proceed analysis from jean`

Activate `JEAN_REVIEW` for the current task/revision.

- Jean is the authoritative formal reviewer for this task.
- Claude/Coordinator approval is optional unless Jean explicitly requests it.
- Every subsequent review comment from Jean is a formal finding with ID `USR-REV-NNN`.
- Record the exact comment, severity if stated, affected requirement/business flow, affected files/classes/methods when known, Codex response, resulting change, revision, and status.
- Valid statuses: `OPEN`, `ADDRESSED - AWAITING JEAN`, `ACCEPTED BY JEAN`, `REJECTED BY JEAN`, `WAIVED BY JEAN`.
- Codex may mark a finding `ADDRESSED - AWAITING JEAN`; Codex MUST NOT close, accept, reject, or waive Jean's finding.
- Material design/code changes made because of Jean's comments remain under Jean review.
- Jean may still request `proceed claude`, `proceed claude deep`, or `proceed coordinator`; those are advisory/formal team paths only when Jean requests them.
- AI agents must still surface critical risks they discover; JEAN_REVIEW removes mandatory AI approval, not engineering warnings.

## Command: `proceed merging from jean`

This is Jean's explicit authorization to implement the exact Jean-reviewed scope into the LOCAL working tree.

Before implementation, verify:
- `JEAN_REVIEW` applies to the current task.
- the exact reviewed revision/specification is identifiable and frozen by revision/hash when practical;
- no `OPEN` Jean findings remain;
- no `ADDRESSED - AWAITING JEAN` findings remain;
- implementation scope, required logging, tests, validation and registry items are known.

If any gate fails: DO NOT IMPLEMENT; report the blocker to Jean.

When all gates pass:
1. Freeze/record the exact Jean-approved revision/hash.
2. Implement only the approved scope in the local working tree.
3. Add all agreed logging/observability.
4. Run required tests and validation, including Replay/Production parity where applicable.
5. Reconcile actual changes against the approved registry/specification.
6. Update required local documentation/registries.
7. Produce implementation/validation evidence.
8. Stop and report results to Jean.

`proceed merging from jean` DOES NOT authorize: Git push, remote merge, deployment, Production change, database write, or unrelated refactoring.

If a material deviation from Jean's approved design is required, STOP implementation and return to `JEAN_REVIEW` for Jean's decision.

Explicitly distinguish tables actually queried successfully from tables merely mentioned or considered. Never fabricate query execution or log coverage.

===== agent-docs/AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md =====
# AGREED KNOWLEDGE

BOTH Codex and Claude. Existing and future entries must pass the dual-agent

===== agent-docs/DATABASE_INVESTIGATION_RULES.md =====
# Database investigation rules

Coordinator database: `crypto_ai` on localhost:3307 as `ai_investigator`.
Do not mix with `crypto_ai_v2`.

## Credentials
Agents never receive the database password.
The coordinator/wrapper reads `AGENT_DB_PASSWORD` from the local environment only.
Do not write that value into AGENTS.md, COORDINATOR.md, Cursor rules, docs, prompts, logs, or source.

Other settings (defaults in parentheses):

- `AGENT_DB_HOST` (localhost)
- `AGENT_DB_PORT` (3307)
- `AGENT_DB_NAME` (crypto_ai)
- `AGENT_DB_USER` (ai_investigator)
- `AGENT_MYSQL_EXE` (local mysql.exe)

## Approved access path
Agents request evidence. The coordinator executes:

`scripts/agent-db-query.ps1`

The MySQL account is database-level read-only. The wrapper is an additional safety layer.

Allowed statement families: `SELECT`, `SHOW`, `DESCRIBE`/`DESC`, `EXPLAIN`.
Everything else is rejected by default, including writes, DDL, grants, CALL, SET, LOAD, locks, and transaction control.
Multiple statements are allowed only if the wrapper proves each statement independently read-only.

## Audit
Each query is stored under `agent-docs/runs/<run-id>/db/` with SQL, result, and `DATABASE_QUERY_LOG.md`.
Never log the password.

## Time
Raw MySQL/Binance timestamps are UTC. Never describe a raw DB timestamp as KSA.
When converting for a report:

- UTC: `<raw time>`
- KSA: `<UTC + 3h>`

## Registry
`agent-docs/DATABASE_ENTITY_REGISTRY.md` is durable entity knowledge.
One agent's discovery is not fact. Append only through dual-agent agreement (`AGREED`/`AGREED`).
Proposed future schema is not current schema.

## Scope examples
Harmless reads may cover `trade_signal`, trades/positions, candles, decision/analysis/market-context tables, Replay/test identifiers, and related trader entities in `crypto_ai` only.

### DB_REQUEST
QueryId: DBQ-CODEX-I1-001  
Purpose: Inspect the actual `crypto_ai` schema for the bounded trade-reconstruction entities before selecting data columns or asserting relationships for frozen paper-position IDs 182, 172, 162, 158, 143, and 137.  
SQL:
```sql
SELECT
    c.table_name,
    c.ordinal_position,
    c.column_name,
    c.column_type,
    c.is_nullable,
    c.column_key,
    c.extra
FROM information_schema.columns AS c
WHERE c.table_schema = 'crypto_ai'
  AND c.table_name IN (
      'paper_position',
      'wallet_trade',
      'trade_signal',
      'signal_processing_work',
      'market_price_event',
      'managed_position',
      'candle',
      'technical_indicator'
  )
ORDER BY c.table_name, c.ordinal_position;
```
### END_DB_REQUEST

===== iteration03-codex.txt =====
## FINDINGS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** All six frozen `paper_position` rows are closed with negative P&L. Their lifecycle timing is:

| ID | Symbol | Held | Closed UTC | Closed KSA |
|---:|---|---:|---|---|
| 182 | ACEUSDT | 00:08:14.009789 | 2026-10-12 10:05:20.486035 | 2026-10-12 13:05:20.486035 |
| 172 | SHIBUSDT | 00:39:40.397141 | 2026-10-12 07:15:45.484258 | 2026-10-12 10:15:45.484258 |
| 162 | DOGEUSDT | 00:23:57.701460 | 2026-10-12 04:26:04.116278 | 2026-10-12 07:26:04.116278 |
| 158 | XRPUSDT | 00:55:01.826646 | 2026-10-12 03:30:06.638124 | 2026-10-12 06:30:06.638124 |
| 143 | EGLDUSDT | 00:03:31.371462 | 2026-10-11 22:43:36.229162 | 2026-10-12 01:43:36.229162 |
| 137 | EGLDUSDT | 00:05:07.611462 | 2026-10-11 21:00:12.121700 | 2026-10-12 00:00:12.121700 |

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** For #158, log event `1912742` records an XRPUSDT price callback as `APPLIED` with `observedUtc=2026-10-12T03:30:06.322Z`; the position closes 316.124 ms later. This is a close temporal association, not yet proof that this event exited position #158.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** For #182, log event `2007194` records ACEUSDT as `APPLIED` at `observedUtc=2026-10-12T10:05:20.431Z`; #182 closes 55.035 ms later. Again, association is strong but not a durable event-to-position link.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** #162 closes at `2026-10-12 04:26:04.116278 UTC`. DOGEUSDT signal `172875` is only recorded as created at `04:26:04.269527 UTC`, 153.249 ms after the close; it therefore cannot have caused #162ƒ?Ts closure. The saved position instead names `exit_signal_id=168277`, which remains unverified.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** At #182ƒ?Ts close, the ACEUSDT 1m candle ending `10:04:59.999 UTC` begins worker handling at `10:05:20.197 UTC`; its 1m signal is inserted at `10:05:30.284 UTC`. The worker delay is about 20.2 seconds to generation and 30.3 seconds to insertion. The post-close signal cannot explain the recorded close at `10:05:20.486 UTC`.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The source explicitly permits an `APPLIED` price outcome to represent a successful no-op when no open position exists. Therefore neither price callback above establishes the recorded exit without matching position/wallet/audit evidence.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Exact-second log searches did not find a symbol-bearing close line for #137, #143, or #172. This is a correlation limitation, not evidence that no exit occurred.

- **EXTERNAL RESEARCH:** None.

## EVIDENCE

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `DBQ-CODEX-I2-001-CODEX-result.txt` supplies the lifecycle timestamps, exit reasons, and signal IDs from authoritative `crypto_ai.paper_position`.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `logs/crypto-ai-trader.2026-10-12.3.log.gz` contains XRP event `1912742`; `logs/crypto-ai-trader.2026-10-12.7.log.gz` contains ACE event `2007194` and the delayed ACE analysis stages.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** [MarketPriceEventService.java](/C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/market/service/MarketPriceEventService.java:84) records `PRICE_OUTCOME_COMMITTED` only after the effects transaction commits. [LivePositionProtectionService.java](/C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/LivePositionProtectionService.java:70) confirms that successful no-op callbacks receive the same `APPLIED / PROTECTION_CALLBACK_COMPLETED` outcome.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** [PaperTradingService.java](/C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/service/PaperTradingService.java:501) sets `closed_at` during terminal close processing, but current evidence does not identify the exact calling path for each frozen position.

## UNKNOWNS

- Exact, durable links among each `paper_position`, its `market_price_event`, managed/wallet position, wallet execution, and exit audit row.
- The price and threshold evaluation for each suspected exit-trigger event.
- Whether #182ƒ?Ts delayed candle analysis breached an applicable freshness rule; the configured threshold and contemporaneous execution path are not yet evidenced.
- The actual `trade_signal` rows for entry IDs `176710, 171589, 167668, 165412, 159408, 156723` and exit ID `168277`.
- Whether current DB rows reproduce the frozen snapshot state rather than later state.

### DB_REQUEST
QueryId: DBQ-CODEX-I3-001  
Purpose: Inspect the actual schema before selecting timing/causality fields for the six frozen positions. This identifies durable event, wallet execution, audit, and signal tables that can prove or refute the provisional log-to-close associations.  
SQL:
```sql
SELECT
    c.table_name,
    c.ordinal_position,
    c.column_name,
    c.column_type,
    c.is_nullable,
    c.column_key,
    c.extra
FROM information_schema.columns AS c
WHERE c.table_schema = 'crypto_ai'
  AND c.table_name IN (
      'market_price_event',
      'paper_position',
      'wallet_trade',
      'wallet_managed_position',
      'wallet_execution',
      'position_management_event',
      'production_exit_audit',
      'trade_signal',
      'signal_processing_work'
  )
ORDER BY c.table_name, c.ordinal_position;
```
### END_DB_REQUEST

## FINDINGS

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** Current source has a distinct live-price protection route for all five frozen positions with `exit_signal_id = NULL` (#182, #172, #158, #143, #137). It closes `paper_position` only after `WalletAutoExecutionService.executeMechanicalExit(...)` returns true, then writes the position close and immutable exit audit in the same transaction path. [LivePositionProtectionService.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/LivePositionProtectionService.java:235)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The current route-compatible classifications are:

| Position | Stored close reason | Stored exit signal | Current route-compatible condition |
|---:|---|---:|---|
| 182 ACEUSDT | `SELL_CONFIRMED` | NULL | bearish 1m + bearish 5m + non-bullish/missing 1h |
| 172 SHIBUSDT | `HTF_SELL_CONFIRMED` | NULL | bearish 1h + 5m not supportive |
| 158 XRPUSDT | `SELL_CONFIRMED` | NULL | bearish 1m + bearish 5m + non-bullish/missing 1h |
| 143 EGLDUSDT | `PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT` | NULL | profit lock triggered, then price fell below the hard protected-profit floor |
| 137 EGLDUSDT | `SELL_CONFIRMED` | NULL | bearish 1m + bearish 5m + non-bullish/missing 1h |
| 162 DOGEUSDT | `STRONG_SELL` | 168277 | signal-processing route: validated 1m `STRONG_SELL` |

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `PositionExitPolicy` is the sole current source of `SELL_CONFIRMED` and `HTF_SELL_CONFIRMED`. It requires the conditions above; a 1m pullback alone is deliberately insufficient. [PositionExitPolicy.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/PositionExitPolicy.java:20)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** #162 is materially different. Its populated `exit_signal_id=168277`, stored `STRONG_SELL` reason, and exit explanation that is formatted as a signal explanation are compatible with `PaperTradingService` closing directly from a validated SELL/STRONG_SELL signal. That route writes the signal as `exitSignal`; it requires a 1m bearish signal plus fresh bearish 5m and fresh non-bullish 1h context. [PaperTradingService.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/service/PaperTradingService.java:197) [TradeExecutionValidationService.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/service/TradeExecutionValidationService.java:165)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** #143ƒ?Ts saved explanation is numerically consistent with the current live hard-floor branch: `4.033000000000 A- 1.0005 = 4.035016500000`, exactly the recorded hard protected-profit floor; its exit price was `4.027000000000`, below that floor. Current code applies that branch only after profit-lock triggering. [LivePositionProtectionService.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/LivePositionProtectionService.java:207)

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** For all six rows, `realized_pnl` matches `quantity A- (exit_price ƒ^' entry_price)` to the persisted 12-decimal rounding tolerance. This is consistent with the current terminal paper-position formula and does not indicate fees or an unaccounted partial exit in the authoritative position P&L. [PaperTradingService.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/service/PaperTradingService.java:495) [LivePositionProtectionService.java](C:/workspace/crypto-ai-trader-mysql/src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/LivePositionProtectionService.java:395)

- **AGENT INFERENCE:** The #182 and #158 near-synchronous `APPLIED` price callbacks from Iteration 3 are route-compatible with their `SELL_CONFIRMED` closes, but remain unproven as causes. `APPLIED` can represent a successful no-position/no-op callback, so a durable position/wallet/audit link is required.

- **AGENT INFERENCE:** The source evidence supports a preliminary split: #162 likely exited via persisted-signal processing; the other five are more consistent with live-price protection. This is not yet a historical-runtime finding because the deployed revision/configuration at the October 12 UTC events is not evidenced.

QueryId: DBQ-CODEX-I4-001  
Purpose: Inspect the actual `crypto_ai` schema before requesting route-specific records. This is needed to construct bounded joins that can prove or refute the provisional signal-route versus live-protection-route classifications for frozen positions only.  
SQL:
```sql
SELECT
    c.table_name,
    c.ordinal_position,
    c.column_name,
    c.column_type,
    c.is_nullable,
    c.column_key,
    c.extra
FROM information_schema.columns AS c

      'paper_position',

      'production_exit_audit',
      'market_price_event',
      'position_management_event',
      'position_analysis',
      'signal_processing_work'
  )
ORDER BY c.table_name, c.ordinal_position;
```

```sql
FROM crypto_ai.wallet_trade AS wt
WHERE wt.signal_id IN (176710, 171589, 167668, 165412, 159408, 156723)
ORDER BY wt.signal_id, wt.executed_at, wt.id;
```

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md =====
   1: # Crypto AI Trader ƒ?" Architecture
   2: 
   3: ## FIX-156 Replay boundary correction ƒ?" 2026-10-16
   4: 
   5: Replay has a dedicated `com.crypto.regression.replay` boundary foundation. It
   6: holds the frozen policy-profile contract, Replay-only sizing/minimum-order/SELL
   7: policy primitives, and an operation-aware gateway for the narrowly permitted
   8: `fix122_evaluation` diagnostic operations. This foundation remains incomplete:
   9: the existing shadow Replay workflow has not yet been fully rewired away from
  10: wallet settings, live Binance constraints, shared BUY validation, or legacy
  11: test-table output. Production runtime and trading behavior remain unchanged.
  12: 
  13: V110 was confirmed already applied and was restored unchanged. Any new
  14: Replay-owned schema is forward-only V111; it creates profile-detail, run
  15: certificate, and price-coverage records without changing an existing table.
  16: 
  17: The active baseline no longer reads the legacy shared `fix122_replay_revision`
  18: experiment switch, and its manual chart does not query `wallet_trade`. These
  19: are boundary corrections only; the Replay-local BUY/lifecycle mirror and all
  20: parity evidence remain pending.
  21: 
  22: `ReplaySizingPolicy`, `ReplayMinimumExecutionPolicy`, and the existing
  23: `ReplaySellValidationPolicy` are Spring-managed Replay-only components because
  24: they are injected by `ShadowProductionReplayService`; they have no wallet or
  25: live-Binance dependency.
  26: 
  27: Status labels used in this document:
  28: 
  29: - **Current** ƒ?" present in the repository today.
  30: - **Pending** ƒ?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
  31: - **Legacy/shared** ƒ?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
  32:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
  33: 
  34: ## Current release status
  35: 
  36: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
  37: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
  38: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
  39: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
  40: 
  41: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
  42: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
  43: 
  44: **FIX-156 is in progress and Replay-only.** A foundation exists for immutable
  45: Replay policy profiles, Replay-owned evidence/output tables, and a fail-closed
  46: Replay SQL allow-list. The existing Replay service has not yet been rewired
  47: away from its live wallet/Binance/shared-service dependencies, so FIX-156 is
  48: not complete or deployable.
  49: 
  50: ## System purpose
  51: 
  52: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
  53: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
  54: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
  55: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
  56: 
  57: ## Runtime and data ownership
  58: 
  59: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
  60: |---|---|---|
  61: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
  62: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
  63: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
  64: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
  65: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
  66: 
  67: ## Current primary flow
  68: 
  69: ```text
  70: Binance WebSocket kline
  71:   -> BinanceKlineService
  72:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
  73:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
  74:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
  75:       -> observer transaction
  76:       -> close-dispatch transaction
  77:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  78:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  79:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  80:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  81:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  82:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  83:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
  84: ```
  85: 
  86: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
  87: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
  88: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
  89: 
  90: ## Current core persistence
  91: 
  92: | Table/domain | Purpose |
  93: |---|---|
  94: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
  95: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
  96: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
  97: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
  98: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
  99: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
 100: 
 101: ## Legacy/shared-market path
 102: 
 103: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
 104: 
 105: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
 106: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
 107: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
 108: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
 109: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
 110: 
 111: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
 112: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
 113: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
 114: 
 115: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
 116: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
 117: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
 118: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
 119: 
 120: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
 121: 
 122: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
 123: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
 124: path must never run together.
 125: 
 126: Implemented safeguards:
 127: 
 128: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
 129:   closed candle.
 130: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
 131:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
 132: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
 133:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
 134: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
 135:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
 136: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
 137:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
 138: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
 139: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
 140:   query retired shared-market tables.
 141: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
 142:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
 143:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
 144:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
 145:   candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
 146:   the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
 147: - Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
 148:   boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
 149:   not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
 150:   analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
 151:   repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
 152:   gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
 153:   provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
 154:   and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
 155:   case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
 156:   not proof that the gap is closed.
 157: - Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
 158:   duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
 159:   REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
 160:   terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
 161:   a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
 162:   failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
 163: - The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
 164:   not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
 165:   queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
 166:   protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
 167: - Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
 168:   V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
 169:   pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
 170:   request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
 171:   unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
 172: - Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
 173:   protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
 174: - Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
 175:   database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
 176:   separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
 177:   HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
 178:   General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
 179:   clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
 180:   Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
 181:   transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
 182:   REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
 183: - An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
 184:   quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
 185:   the controlled drain window.
 186: 
 187: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
 188: 
 189: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
 190: 
 191: ```text
 192: PENDING
 193:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
 194:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
 195:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
 196: ```
 197: 
 198: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
 199: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
 200: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
 201: 
 202: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
 203: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
 204: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
 205: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
 206: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
 207: 
 208: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
 209: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
 210: 
 211: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
 212: 
 213: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
 214: 
 215: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
 216: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
 217: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
 218: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
 219: remains unchanged.
 220: 
 221: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
 222: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
 223: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
 224: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
 225: 
 226: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
 227: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
 228: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
 229: 
 230: ## Deployment posture
 231: 
 232: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
 233: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
 234: 
 235: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
 236: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
 237: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
 238: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
 239: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
 240: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
 241: compile the Java 21 project.
 242: 
 243: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
 244: without explicit user approval.
 245: 
 246: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
 247: 
 248: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
 249: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
 250: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
 251: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
 252: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
 253: 
 254: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
 255: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
 256: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
 257: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
 258: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
 259: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
 260: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
 261: must stay `false`.
 262: 
 263: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
 264: contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
 265: mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
 266: starting.
 267: 
 268: ### Shared Binance REST admission
 269: 
 270: `BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
 271: immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
 272: response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
 273: the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
 274: calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
 275: of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.
 276: 
 277: ### Outcome-schema startup invariant
 278: 
 279: The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
 280: terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
 281: though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
 282: runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
 283: table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
 284: when they attempt their first outcome write.
 285: # FIX-154 implementation update ƒ?" 2026-10-10
 286: 
 287: `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
 288: the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
 289: entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
 290: ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
 291: for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
 292: uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
 293: initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
 294: the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.
 295: 
 296: `buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
 297: best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
 298: execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
 299: be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
 300: existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
 301: that guard has already rejected an add.
 302: 
 303: `GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
 304: blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.
 305: 
 306: ## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation ƒ?" enabled 2026-10-10
 307: 
 308: In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
 309: five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
 310: missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
 311: the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
 312: An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
 313: plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
 314: dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
 315: ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

===== codex-docs/TRADE_INVESTIGATION_AGENT.md =====
   1: # Trade Investigation Agent
   2: 
   3: ## Role
   4: 
   5: Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
   6: happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
   7: rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
   8: at that time.
   9: 
  10: Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
  11: database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.
  12: 
  13: ## Required input
  14: 
  15: Obtain or resolve:
  16: 
  17: - Environment and application: local, test, or production.
  18: - Database host, port, and schema.
  19: - One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
  20:   a bounded approximate entry-time window.
  21: - Optional question or suspected issue.
  22: 
  23: Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
  24: level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
  25: columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
  26: `crypto_ai_v2`.
  27: 
  28: ## Evidence rules
  29: 
  30: - Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
  31: - Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
  32: - Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
  33: - Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
  34: - Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
  35:   conflicting evidence**.
  36: - Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
  37: - A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
  38:   transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
  39:   committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
  40:   no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
  41: - Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
  42:   no configuration history exists, report that limitation.
  43: 
  44: ## Investigation workflow
  45: 
  46: ### 1. Resolve identity and environment
  47: 
  48: Link the trade reference to, where available:
  49: 
  50: - `paper_position`
  51: - `wallet_trade`
  52: - entry and exit `trade_signal`
  53: - `signal_processing_work`
  54: - wallet/position execution records
  55: 
  56: Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
  57: local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.
  58: 
  59: ### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle
  60: 
  61: Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
  62: requires it. Collect:
  63: 
  64: | Evidence | Establishes |
  65: | --- | --- |
  66: | `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
  67: | `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
  68: | `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
  69: | `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
  70: | `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
  71: | `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
  72: | configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
  73: | application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |
  74: 
  75: Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.
  76: 
  77: ### 3. Reconstruct the timeline
  78: 
  79: Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:
  80: 
  81: `candle closed ƒ+' signal saved ƒ+' work claimed ƒ+' locks acquired ƒ+' entry committed ƒ+' protection observations ƒ+' exit decision ƒ+' exit committed`
  82: 
  83: Measure separately:
  84: 
  85: - candle close to signal generation;
  86: - signal generation to processing/entry;
  87: - observation time to protection completion;
  88: - exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.
  89: 
  90: Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
  91: or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.
  92: 
  93: ### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality
  94: 
  95: Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:
  96: 
  97: - signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
  98: - final decision and entry-allowed result;
  99: - BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
 100:   strategy gates that applied at that time;
 101: - correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
 102: - quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.
 103: 
 104: Return two separate conclusions:
 105: 
 106: - **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
 107: - **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
 108:   evidence.
 109: 
 110: Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
 111: as information the entry decision had.
 112: 
 113: ### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior
 114: 
 115: For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
 116: `PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
 117: gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
 118: evidence.
 119: 
 120: For each relevant accepted observation:
 121: 
 122: 1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
 123: 2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
 124: 3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
 125:    wallet execution record.
 126: 4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
 127:    lock waits, or pool pressure.
 128: 
 129: Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
 130: take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.
 131: 
 132: ### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality
 133: 
 134: Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
 135: Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
 136: the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
 137: the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.
 138: 
 139: ### 7. Reconcile financial results
 140: 
 141: Recalculate using execution records:
 142: 
 143: - bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
 144: - gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
 145: - partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.
 146: 
 147: Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.
 148: 
 149: ### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully
 150: 
 151: Classify findings as one or more of:
 152: 
 153: - strategy decision;
 154: - data freshness or coverage;
 155: - analysis delay;
 156: - execution failure;
 157: - protection failure;
 158: - database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
 159: - accounting/UI discrepancy;
 160: - insufficient evidence.
 161: 
 162: Run Replay only as a controlled comparison with equivalent candles, accepted
 163: prices, strategy version, and settings. State every unavailable input or
 164: material difference. Never claim parity by substituting candle OHLC for missing
 165: price evidence or treating `PENDING` as accepted.
 166: 
 167: ## Required final report
 168: 
 169: 1. **Trade identity and environment** ƒ?" IDs, symbol, version/mode, scope.
 170: 2. **UTC/KSA timeline** ƒ?" evidence-backed sequence and latency breakdown.
 171: 3. **Entry** ƒ?" execution correctness and entry quality.
 172: 4. **Exit** ƒ?" execution correctness and exit quality.
 173: 5. **Stop-loss/profit-lock** ƒ?" worked, failed, or unverifiable, with evidence.
 174: 6. **P&L reconciliation** ƒ?" quantities, prices, fees, and discrepancies.
 175: 7. **Root cause and confidence** ƒ?" confirmed defect, valid loss, configuration
 176:    issue, delay, or insufficient evidence.
 177: 8. **Evidence references** ƒ?" record IDs, query/log timestamps, code/config
 178:    references.
 179: 9. **Missing evidence** ƒ?" exactly what prevents a stronger conclusion.
 180: 10. **Recommended correction and regression checks** ƒ?" only when justified.
 181: 
 182: A profitable trade can violate the rules, and a losing trade can be a correct
 183: execution. State those independently.

===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
   1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
   2: 
   3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
   4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
   5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
   6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
   7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
   8:   capped at 25%.
   9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
  10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  11:   never authorizes an add.
  12: 
  13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
  14: 
  15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  16:   the data-quality gate.
  17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
  20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  21:   generation after a repaired quality window.

  65: # 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 exchange/database clock correction
  66: 
  67: - Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
  68:   too early or too old during freshness checks.
  69: - Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
  70:   database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
  71:   callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
  72: - The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
  73:   dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
  74: - General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
  75:   scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
  76: - Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
  77:   the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.

  95: # SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10
  96: 
  97: - Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  98:   This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
  99: - Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
 100:   continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
 101: - Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
 102:   evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
 103:   to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
 104: - Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
 105:   evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
 106:   hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
 107:   1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
 108:   target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
 109:   drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
 110:   exit eligibility.
 111: - Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
 112:   or near-TP distance alone.
 113: - Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
 114:   with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
 115:   1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
 116:   protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
 117:   lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
 118: - Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
 119:   paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
 120:   comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
 121:   gross profit is a net gain.
 122: - Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
 123:   unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
 124:   near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
 125:   calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
 126:   pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
 127:   qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
 128:   a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
 129:   momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
 130:   and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
 131:   false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
 132:   reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
 133: - Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
 134:   entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
 135:   distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
 136:   evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
 137:   intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
 138:   floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
 139:   that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
 140:   improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
 141:   viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
 142: - Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
 143:   85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
 144:   quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
 145:   current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
 146:   unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
 147:   This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
 148:   Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
 149:   B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
 150:   must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
 151:   tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
 152:   use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
 153: - Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
 154:   from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
 155:   high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
 156:   `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
 157:   breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
 158:   fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
 159:   numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
 160:   length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
 161:   before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
 162:   out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
 163: - Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
 164:   before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
 165:   Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
 166:   order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
 167:   conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
 168:   with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
 169:   `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
 170:   breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
 171:   breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
 172:   avoided by A.

 174: # Replay-versus-Production parity audit Г?" 2026-10-10
 175: 
 176: - Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
 177:   the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
 178:   database data, or Replay result was changed.
 179: - Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
 180:   `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
 181:   through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
 182:   `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
 183:   profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
 184:   classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
 185:   consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
 186:   visible, matching the documented live ordering.
 187: - Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
 188:   identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
 189:   manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
 190:   position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
 191:   reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
 192:   `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
 193:   real execution latency.
 194: - Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
 195:   persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
 196:   context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
 197:   stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
 198:   whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
 199:   are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
 200:   required historical evidence exists and is reported.
 201: - Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
 202:   `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
 203:   does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
 204:   protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
 205:   are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
 206:   should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
 207: - Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
 208:   parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
 209:   Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
 210:   Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
 211:   price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
 212: - Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
 213:   and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
 214:   evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
 215:   to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
 216:   scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
 217:   release-equivalence certificate.

   1: # FIX-154 ƒ?" Trusted higher-timeframe BUY validation
   2: 
   3: Status: implementation design approved. FIX-154 is scheduled before the
   4: remaining FIX-153 runtime release work.
   5: 
   6: ## Purpose and boundary
   7: 
   8: FIX-154 replaces the current binary fresh-1h bearish veto with a trusted
   9: higher-timeframe assessment. It does not relax the existing 5m confirmation
  10: requirement, risk/reward result, current-1m BTC protection, or profile rules
  11: except where a raw 1h decision would otherwise act as an untrusted veto.
  12: 
  13: The legacy `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md` is an unrelated shared-market
  14: readiness design and is not an implementation source for this fix.
